# Consolidated changes against witnesses C and H

All readings are decided on the supplied scans. The source-location references below are cote/scan, physical line or region, and an original-image pixel box. C and H are independent AI drafts, not authorities. Retained doubts remain in the page reports and unresolved lists. Certainty-only decisions from completed audits have separate per-page sections in the notes; they do not appear as changed-reading table rows or enter the change totals. Pure macro or editorial metadata differences are excluded.

## Corrections affecting mathematical content

- **29/115, A01, 7:** The identity of the covering variable is visually unresolved. Later V occurrences provide context but do not establish the order of this overstrike.
- **29/115, A05, 10–11:** The insertion specifies an open neighbourhood T in the Grassmannian; H relocates it and adds words/symbols not present on the sheet.
- **29/116, R116-01, 1:** The distinction concerns the alphabet or an earlier canceled letter of the target space. No mathematical correction is made.
- **29/116, R116-08, 10:** H omits the barred-eta index on Y, losing the explicitly written fiber specification.
- **29/119, R008, 11–12:** The argument of the induced fundamental-group map is affected; the local text elsewhere uses V, but that does not settle the overstrike order.
- **29/120, R120-15, 20:** The transcription retains the bare G actually typed, rather than adding the stabilizer subscript that H supplies.
- **29/121, A03, 7:** H supplies a pullback star which is not on the sheet. The wording image inverse does not authorize adding it.
- **29/121, A06, 10; H subscript in diagram:** The point under H is eta, distinct in shape from the Latin y used in the later added condition.
- **29/121, A14, 14, right product:** The right product has an explicit double-prime decoration. No interpretation or normalization of its range is imposed.
- **29/123, R123-19, 18–20:** The source leaves a precise statement of functoriality to be formulated; H replaces that explicit qualification with a different sentence.
- **29/123, R123-21, 21–29:** H omits the whole return to no. 5 and its refinement using Tate-module homomorphisms.
- **29/123, R123-26, 28–29:** These are the composed homomorphisms whose classes the last sentence declares conjugate.
- **29/123, R123-27, 30:** The concluding conjugacy assertion is entirely absent from H.
- **29/125, A21, L29:** The final category in condition (iii) is underlined G, not underlined C: the stationarity condition is stated for an object of the category of groups.
- **29/128, R128-21, 26, whole formula:** H adds an unwritten direct-image-style subscript and changes the alphabet. Neither is supplied from the expected mathematics.
- **29/128, R128-26, above 29 and above 30:** The relative-group notation belongs to the pro-scheme, while filtrant qualifies the projective system.
- **32/008, C32-008-27, M6, after premier à:** The final condition retains Y as primary with zeta as an explicit alternative; no mathematical expectation resolves that symbol.
- **32/010, R005, 8:** The letter identifying the quasi-finite morphism has visible g/f layers; no mathematical normalization to f is made.
- **32/013, R019, 19:** The base symbol retains both visible layers instead of being normalized by its role in the sentence.
- **32/013, R024, 23:** The sign is strict-less-than primary, with non-strict-less-than secondary; neither is H non-equality. Formal degree checks use the primary less-than and2 reading conditionally, not as proof of the ink.
- **32/013, R029, 23, coefficient after the inequality:** The overlapping coefficient is preserved locally. The earlier formal comparison of degree bounds is conditional on reading the live coefficient as2.
- **32/014, R014-14, 18, complete defining expression:** The tall bent sign is the dimension variable delta, not H’s uncertain c; the written numerical value and d-d-prime order remain unchanged.
- **32/014, R014-16, 22, complete zero condition:** The tall bent sign is the dimension variable delta, not H’s uncertain c; the written numerical value and d-d-prime order remain unchanged.
- **32/014, R014-19, 24, complete unit condition:** The tall bent sign is the dimension variable delta, not H’s uncertain c; the written numerical value and d-d-prime order remain unchanged.
- **32/015, R015-18, 23, lower-right diagram object:** The lower-right pullback letter has both f and g layers, so the live-letter choice is not silently regularized.
- **32/015, R015-22, 25:** The reference contains overlapping5 and9/4-compatible material; the typing order and original numeral are not settled from the intended reference.

### Remaining uncertainties

- 7: `\mathbb{U}`. Rounded U contour plus internal diagonals are certain, but correction order versus deliberate double strike is not. Scope: this occurrence only; no other page was consulted.
- 7: `tel que sa,`. The tall strokes, q descender and following letter remnants support this phrase; the dense x overlay prevents certainty for all letter interiors. The comma itself is visible.
- 11: `T`. Small raised capital is covered by the crossing and insertion leader. Crossbar/stem favour T, but a curved left remnant admits G.
- 14: `codi\deleted{emen}mension`. Independent B012 includes four overtyped cells, not three: rounded e bodies and m/n uprights extend through the final s-position. Both whole-layer orders remain uncertain; codi and ion are secure.
- 14: `comp\deleted{t}e`. A second transverse stroke appears on t, but it could be an overstrike rather than a purposeful cancellation. State retained as C doubt.
- 14: `m`. Very cramped handwritten addition above the end of compte has m-like humps, but a joined nt or n is still possible. Its leader reaches the final-letter area; the precise letter-level attachment remains unresolved.
- 23: `connu`. C/o contours and later stems are visible under x overtyping; not every central join is independently clear. No complete alternate word is supported.
- 8: `en\deleted{g}visagée`. B006 separates the full g loop and v arms, but not which machine impression was last. The primary correction remains g-to-v.
- 15–16: `croise\`{m}ents`. B015 has a secure grave above the m cell and a dense rounded inner body. The original accented-m reading remains primary, but an accented-letter/m overstrike cannot be excluded.
- 17: `\deleted{$V$ de $Y$}`. B016 favours open V arms, but the lower portion that would distinguish a U bottom lies under cancellation ink.

### Remaining uncertainties

- 1: `\mathbb{P}`. Crowded upper bowl and paired interior strokes versus a possible lower R leg. This is the only capital with this ambiguity on this page; no recurring-scope extrapolation is made. Independent B001 also permits reverse P/R chronology; the crowded diagonal does not identify the last impression.
- 6: `\deleted{n}i`. A distinct dot sits over two crowded uprights in one character position. The earlier n remains possible but is not separable from a repeated or displaced i.
- 8: `ella`. The last composite letter retains an a-like bowl and foot. The middle uprights and pen loop permit the replacement elle, but the live and earlier strokes cannot be separated. Context is not used to suppress the visible a-like component.
- 8: `vrai\handwritten{e}`. The e-shaped penned body is filled; B003 does not distinguish a retraced live e from cancellation of e. Both letter identity and state remain uncertain.
- 10: `\deleted{,}`. The lowered position and descending tail favour a canceled comma. The dense crossing covers the original glyph and does not eliminate s.
- 4: `\deleted{ce n'est pas,}`. Independent B002: a rounded lower contour supports c, but a tall spur merged with the cancellation leaves l possible. This is an added doubt, not a changed primary reading.

### Remaining uncertainties

- 4: `f'(gx')=`. The x-overtyping conceals the inner x/prime and equality endings; the candidate is retained as a whole formula, not silently completed.
- 10: `;`. A descending punctuation tail survives after b, but the expected upper dot of a semicolon is obscured by cancellation.
- 10: `un tel`. Residual upright/curve patterns support these short words; several interior strokes coincide with overtyped x's.
- 10: `f'`. An f stem and raised mark are visible, but the second character is heavily overtyped.
- 10: `est`. Three positions and final t ascender favor est over C's ainsi, but the inner contours are crossed.
- 10: `donc`. C's candidate remains plausible and is preserved; x overtyping prevents every letter being read without interference.
- 10a: `ainsi que φ attached after f', before est`. The inserted row begins above est, but no surviving caret establishes an exact attachment. The words and their cancelled state are clear. The doubt wrapper around the insertion in the page marks placement only; the word reading ainsi que φ is not doubtful.
- 11–12: `\deleted{B}V`. Both B-like bowls and V-like diagonals are visible; the chronology of the two blue impressions cannot be determined securely. The doubt belongs to the whole induced fundamental-group map.
- 16: `un`. The two cancelled positions show a low join and partial arch, but the x overtyping obscures their full shapes.
- 19: `ft`. The second cancelled position's long upright may be t or the combination of an apostrophe with overtyped x.
- 25: `en`. The first struck word appears to occupy two type positions, favoring en, but the thick strike and nearby leader cover the decisive upper contours.
- 1: `\deleted{N}\handwritten{U}`. Independent B002: a blue diagonal and serif remnants are separately visible within the later black U. N is favoured, V remains possible because the lower convergence is covered.
- 15–16: `locali\deleted{\unsure{s}}sé`. The extra type position after locali is x-canceled, with rounded remnants compatible with s. Its right edge is lost.

### Remaining uncertainties

- 5: `\deleted{$\handwritten{\bar}{\unsure{U|D|X}}\unsure{'}$}`. B002 and wider saved crop show a raised right remnant consistent with a prime, in addition to the overbar. The canceled capital is U-like at its base but D/X are not excluded by the x impressions.
- 7: `entendu\deleted{r}`. The extra character is certainly canceled by an x, but its underlying r is not fully separable. The word entendu itself is clear. The blind reading adds s as another contour-compatible alternative beneath the x.
- 11: `marchera`. The handwritten middle joins do not distinguish all the internal letters independently. C and H word doubt is retained.
- 11: `\overline{f'}`. A dark short segment immediately above f prime coincides with the fine insertion leader. The complete repaired crop includes the bars; the segment cannot securely be assigned exclusively to a bar or leader.
- 11, complete continuation formula: `\overline{X'}\longrightarrow\overline{Y'}\times_{\bar Y}\overline{X'}`. The connector has a small upward hook at its right tip, compatible with an arrowhead; its other head arm is not clear. This newly added certainty doubt has no separate change row. All bars and both X primes in the full formula are retained.
- 12: `\deleted{\illegible{1}}`. One handwritten word is physically obscured by dense crossing strokes between entier and sur. No complete alternative is recoverable.
- 14: `\deleted{men}`. Only three canceled positions are visible after stricte before the paper edge; the last is clipped. The anticipated fourth letter is not supplied.
- 22, inner closing position in the rightmost factor: `)`. The parenthesis-shaped stroke overlaps a rounded blue impression. Two closing positions are present, but the inner composite is not securely decomposed. Whole display reviewed.
- 23: `p\deleted{o}lus`. B015 shows one closed low oval around a tall l stem, favouring o/l overprinting rather than m/l. The impression order remains uncertain.
- 9: `p\deleted{p}rolongement`. The second position retains a p descender and r-like upper shoulder, but the shoulder merges with the bowl; sequence and a plain-p reading cannot be securely excluded.
- archive foliation, comment only: `119`. The last digit has an upper curved body and long descending return. The upper closure is faint, so 9 remains primary with 3 possible. No foliation is inserted into the running transcription.

### Remaining uncertainties

- 6: `\deleted{E}D`. The outer D bowl and inner E-like arm coexist; the order of the two impressions cannot be established.
- 7: `a_{c,c'}`. Closed upper eye and lower bowl/foot favor a visually. A dense/overlapping n remains possible. Later prose n is contextual evidence only; it does not settle this first-formula glyph.
- 9: `de rev`. The d/e, intervening blank type cell, and r/e are recoverable; the final v-like remnant remains covered by the cancelling x.
- 12, cancelled old object: `H_\xi`. Capital is heavily hatched. Xi-like lower remnant and the separate faint upward arrow are visible.
- 12, cancelled upper point next to G: `\xi`. Small original point is covered by dark diagonal hatching; the distinct live lower xi does not settle the cancelled layer.
- 12, cancelled arrow label: `N`. Blue overtyping and pen strokes conceal the old label’s contour; the new live phi above/right remains separate.
- margin beside 24: `\bar C=Y^u`. The first body is open-right and C-like; an E middle arm is too faint to establish. The raised mark is rounded/open upward, leaving u, vee or check placement possible. Bar, equals and Y are secure.
- 25: `x\in`. Repeated x overtyping and the horizontal strike obscure the middle of the relation; x-in remains primary, but x-equals is also compatible with the surviving strokes.
- 27: `a`. Blue rounded original label lies under black b. The surviving upper curve is a-like, but the lower original bowl is concealed.
- added a), final word: `noethériens`. The small body after h is merged with its joining stroke; the raised mark can be an acute or dot. The ending riens and period are clear.

### Remaining uncertainties

- 1, canceled formula: `\handwritten{\unsure{(}} ... \handwritten{\unsure{)}}`. Independent descending/closing curves favor parentheses; the opening top merges with the strike. Words and formula inside the pair are clear.
- 4: `l'image des`. Width and g/final des traces favor the shorter singular phrase, but x and horizontal strikes hide inner letters.
- 9: `dans`. d and final s are visible; x overstrikes obscure the middle positions.
- 12a: `:`. The black mark after Remords has touching lobes.
- 12: `un (canceled)`. Blue u/n-like outlines are overlain by the black replacement; not all underlying strokes are independently readable.
- 12: `des`. Black d is recognizable; the following joined strokes do not separate securely into e/s or u. The grammar does not decide the reading.
- 12: `s after résultat`. Small dark s-like mark touches the t; attachment and shape remain uncertain.
- 16, Pour: `P\unsure{$\overset{\smile}{\underset{\frown}{\mathrm{o}}}$}ur`. The two arcs are visibly centered on o, not on the entire word. Their textual identity remains uncertain.
- 23: `canceled 2 beneath handwritten 3`. Blue descending diagonal and foot favor 2; darker upper two-turn digit is 3. Only the lower reading remains marked doubtful.
- 24: `\underline{\mathbb{Z}}`. Doubled Z or E/Z overtyping remains possible, with no established order.
- 21, inserted word: `Étudions`. After the dotted i the small joined turns admit ions or er; the taller E,t,d and u are clear. The earlier primary is retained with the independent alternative.
- 26, before action: `l\deleted{'}e`. An upright blue remnant occupies the apostrophe/accent height above the live e. Its older-letter status is likely but not certain.
- 27, Tate lower layer: `t`. The lower blue crossbar survives at lower-case-t height, while most of its stem is covered by the new capital T.
- 2, handwritten item label: `b`. The corrected S001 crop shows a closed round body and a short hooked upper stroke. This supports b, but the joined upper part also permits the independent a reading.

### Remaining uncertainties

- 2: `\unsure{\deleted{o}\handwritten{b}|\deleted{o}\handwritten{o}|\deleted{o}\handwritten{6}}n`. The foreground pen ascender and lower return are certain; whether they make a b-like replacement or retrace o is not. The visible typed o is retained as the underlying layer in both candidates. The retracing is also6-shaped; this is now an explicit alternative.
- 6: `r`. Single extra canceled typewriter cell between d and apostrophe; original curve/serif is crossed by the cancellation.
- 6: `\mathbin{-}`. Added doubt to C’s plain connector: a horizontal shaft with a dark terminal nub lacks a distinguishable fork, but an obscured/fused arrowhead is possible.
- 10: `\mathbin{-}`. Canceled f:X–Y formula has a short shaft; original arrowhead cannot be distinguished from the overprinted x diagonals.
- 10: `4`. Right upright and crossbar favor 4, but part of the crossbar/left approach is shared with cancellation; C’s alternatives are retained.
- 12: `à`. Isolated mark has a loop/accent-like top and 2-like sloping body and foot. No secure purpose or superposition order is inferred.
- 14, canceled fragment: `homomo`. Tall initial h and alternating round/humped cells are visible, but x cancellations merge with some middle humps; do not remove the C doubt.
- 14, crowded live-word cell: `\deleted{s}m`. One cell contains s-like and m/n-like impressions before separate e n s cells. The order and m/n identity remain unresolved. The extra final n is preserved.
- 24, initial lower-word cell: `\unsure{\deleted{j}h|\deleted{h}j|\deleted{b}h|\deleted{h}b}`. The extra narrow upright and under-baseline return favour j/h, but its order and the older b/h possibilities remain uncertain.
- 25, final cell of trivial: `\deleted{o}l`. A tall centered l with top serif and lower foot overlaps a rounded impression. The current alternatives retain o/l, b/l and a/l in both orders; o/l remains primary. Expected wording does not settle the layering.
- 25, canceled phrase: `d'où`. The accented final u/n-shaped cell is crossed by x impressions. The following un isomorphisme désignant is readable.

### Remaining uncertainties

- L01: `Vallouin`. The terminal connected strokes and final short mark do not decide in/re/se; the external-letter attribution in H is excluded.
- L01: `6`. Open upper projection and descending curve do not match the immediately preceding closed 8 closely enough to settle the month; no date is inferred.
- L05: `an\unsure{g|p|\deleted{a}g|\deleted{g}a}lytiques`. The g-like lower loop is distinct; the crowded upper cell may also combine a/g. No impression order is secure.
- L21: `s\unsure{\deleted{a}u|\deleted{u}a}ppo\unsure{c|s}e`. The second cell has an extra closed bowl and a u-like lower turn. The penultimate cell is still broken, so its c/s uncertainty is retained.
- L21 cancellation: `finis`. The handwritten fibrés overwrites the middle typed n/i, so not every underlying letter can be isolated.
- L24 cancellation: `de`. A d-like upper hook and e-like lower curve survive; the possible third letter remains concealed by x.
- L28 cancellation: `d'un`. Ascender and apostrophe-like mark support d’un, but u/n interiors remain crossed; the C doubt is not resolved.
- L30 canceled formula: `F:\underline{G}`. The underline is clear. The possible G interior bar is crossed, so the whole canceled formula is kept with both last-capital readings.
- L30 cancellation: `G-groupes`. The prefix capital is under x strokes. Its upper/lower curves do not distinguish G from S, although the hyphen and groupes are recoverable.
- L33: `F`. One dark foreground F is clear; the pale offset contour may be reversed show-through rather than another strike. Scope is only this occurrence before est exact, not the separately inspected ordinary F letters elsewhere.
- L09 cancellation: `pour moi`. The central cells remain crossed by x; their lower strokes do not establish every letter.
- L23 after u,v: `;`. The lower mark has a descending taper, but the worn impression leaves colon possible.

### Remaining uncertainties

- 3: `\underline{\mathbb{C}}`. Heavy duplicated contour is C-shaped; an internal G bar cannot be separated from overlapping ink. This occurrence is not equated silently with the ordinary G elsewhere.
- 4: `le`. The l is clear; x cancellation partly masks e.
- 4: `isomorphe`. Several inner and final letter contours are obscured by x impressions; not every letter is plain. The final x position has no independently separable e contour.
- 6–7: `intuiti\unsure{v}ment`. Only intuiti plus a clipped v-shaped fragment and next-line ment survive. Neither witness's restored e is visible; no missing letter is supplied.
- 10: `pris`. Four crossed cells contain compatible traces, but the p and s cannot be isolated fully.
- 16: `(définie`. An extra initial canceled position contains a tall curved/upright stroke. Parenthesis or t remains unresolved; the word under the following x strokes remains doubtful.
- 18, marginal insertion: `rationnel`. The q descender from the row above crosses early letters. This is an added certainty-only doubt; lorsque and the attachment to opère are secure.
- 19: `qui sera un p-groupe fi`. Type-cell division under the x strokes remains unresolved. A descended p, short horizontal mark and groupe-like cluster favor p-groupe; terminal dotted ink requires more than C's ending. fi is retained as an incomplete visible candidate; no unseen completion to fini is added.
- 23: `\unsure{\deleted{\mathrm{g}}\mathrm{G}|\deleted{\mathrm{G}}\mathrm{g}}\mathrm{al}`. Both lower g and capital G are visible. Which was struck first is not recoverable. Both possible deletion/replacement orders are printed in the local doubt; the later Gal is an ordinary capital G.
- 26: `fini sur lequel`. The cancellation hides some middle contours and the ending.
- 28: `Il est`. Both groups are visible under x strokes but not every base-letter contour is plain.
- 30: `peut-être`. Heavy pen zigzags cover the middle of the typed candidate.

### Remaining uncertainties

- 2: `\deleted{les points}`. The p descender, i dot and t stem survive, but other original strokes merge with x marks and the outer strike.
- 4: `(\mathbb{E}_i)`. Overlapping E/B-like capital; possible ink spread prevents a secure alphabet decision. Full formula retained.
- 6: `\deleted{tel que}`. Not every short original letter is separable from x overtyping.
- 7: `\deleted{Il}`. Two cells, with first upright/serifs partly hidden; second l-like upright is clearer.
- 18, after de: `\deleted{qui}`. Three canceled cells; small internal and dotted strokes do not certify the full word.
- 18, before I: `d\deleted{onc}`. Scope is clear: d live, three following cells canceled. The onc letter sequence remains provisional.
- 20: `\deleted{résidus}`. An accent, a dot and an ascender survive, but the entire canceled word is not recoverable securely.
- 26: `\deleted{de}`. Two original type cells share x marks and the insertion carrier; d/e fragments are visible but not complete.
- 29: `pro-schéma`. Short horizontal at the o/s boundary may be a hyphen or joined letter impressions; no independent full type cell is secure.
- 31–33, each of three base capitals: `\deleted{S}Y`. Distinct Y fork and S body recur. Layer order remains unknown and is marked in place on every occurrence, as requested for these overstrikes.
- 33, right edge: `totau\unsure{x|:}`. Only the last position is doubtful: two partial left terminal strokes survive, without a complete crossing/right side. Two left terminal blobs can also be a colon; the physical right edge remains incomplete.
- 34, second cell: `\deleted{r}e`. The readable e has a raised/shouldered extra stroke, possibly an underlying r. Identity and order of that added layer remain unresolved; there is no separate r position.
- 34, final three cells: `\deleted{ont}`. The first rounded cell is densely crossed; n/t remains and all three cancellation states are visible. No six-letter original word is asserted. The first canceled cell also permits r; its contour cannot be isolated from x.

### Remaining uncertainties

- 8: `de dim`. Typed x impressions cover parts of the e and final m; keep C's canceled-word doubt.
- 10: `\unsure{\deleted{A}S|\deleted{S}A}`. Both S-like and A-like contours occur in one cell; their chronological order is not established.
- 16: `l\unsure{\deleted{h}'|\deleted{'}h}homomorphisme`. A complete extra h body and apostrophe-shaped impression share one cell; uncertain replacement order is shown in place.
- 19: `parfoi\unsure{s}`. The final s is partly clipped, though curved ink remains at the edge.
- 20: `\unsure{\deleted{d}a|\deleted{a}d}\unsure{\deleted{a}p|\deleted{p}a}pelle`. Both first cells contain distinct letter layers. Each order remains independently uncertain.
- 25: `à d`. The first grave/accented a and last tall capped stem are visible under x strokes; the last lower bowl remains partly hidden.
- 28, target H: `\underline{\mathbb{H}}`. An extra right vertical favors doubling but may be a displaced/ghost impression. Only this target is doubtful.
- 28, first canceled index: `Y`. Dense crossing strokes obscure the lower glyph under target H.
- 28, second canceled index: `Y`. The distinct lower glyph after (F_Y) is partly covered; it is not the live inserted Y.
- 28, canceled shift: `[2(d'-d)`. The underlying expression is partly crossed; no separately visible closing square bracket is restored.
- 32: `com\unsure{\deleted{â}p|\deleted{p}â}actifiable`. The p descender and a body overlap beneath a detached circumflex. The accent belongs to the composite cell; its relation to the chronological layers cannot be proved. The â/p encoding retains all ink.
- 31, second cell of déduit: `d\unsure{é|\deleted{a}é|\deleted{é}a}duit`. The second cell has a horizontal e bar with a closed lower bowl/right return that could retain a. Similar offset outlines occur in the adjacent d and i, so repeated impression of é remains primary; a distinct earlier letter and its order are not established.

### Remaining uncertainties

- L01, canceled index: `\deleted{\unsure{!|*}}`. Original lower sign partly covered by later parentheses and crossing strokes; both candidates retained.
- L01, canceled terminal punctuation: `\deleted{\unsure{,|.}}`. The overprinted x obscures whether the old point has a comma tail.
- L04, canceled support: `\deleted{\unsure{!|f}}`. The crossed narrow vertical remnant supports !, while a descending or hooked trace also permits f. The trace cannot be separated securely from the cancellation; neither candidate is resolved.
- L04, clipped closing bracket: `\handwritten{\unsure{]}}`. Corner strokes survive but outer edge is clipped; no missing continuation supplied.
- L05, canceled superscript on Y: `\deleted{\unsure{*|k|''}}`. The densely crossed raised layer permits star, k, or two old quotation strokes. Its original form is unresolved and remains separate from the live paired closing quotation strokes.
- L05, canceled edge group: `\deleted{\unsure{, qui,}}`. The q-like descender, rounded middle and high final point permit the reading, but crossing strokes and edge clipping prevent certainty. The terminal canceled comma tail is now independently visible in audit_deleted_end.
- L11, canceled support: `\deleted{\unsure{!|f}}`. This occurrence was inspected separately. The old point is obscured, and a narrow descending or hooked trace permits f beside ! without resolving the crossed support sign.
- L11, complete (4.2.5) shift: `[2(d'\unsure{\mathord{\perp}|-}d)\unsure{]}`. Short upright rises from a horizontal base. Layer order and intended operator are not inferred from expected shift notation.
- L11, clipped closing bracket: `\handwritten{\unsure{]}}`. Top and lower corner fragments support ], but the right edge is not fully present.
- L13, label final digit: `(4.2.\unsure{6|5})`. Heavy composite has a closed lower bowl and flat/curving upper features; a distinct canceled numeral cannot be isolated.
- L16, whole formula connector: `\mathrm{R}\handwritten{\Gamma}_X(F_X)\handwritten{\unsure{\text{---}|\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{R}\Gamma_Y(F_Y(d'-d))\handwritten{[}2(d'-d)\handwritten{]}.
\tag*{(4.2.8)}
`. The shaft is clear but its tiny terminal nub does not securely establish an arrowhead.
- L20, post-G punctuation: `\unsure{.|:|;}`. Two tiny uneven, slightly diagonal spots; C’s low-dot reading remains first rather than being resolved.
- M3, first word: `\unsure{ou|ne}`. Connected rounded strokes favour ou but permit ne. No grammatical restoration of -il is made.
- L05, article after où: `l\unsure{\deleted{e}a|\deleted{a}e}`. The complete close-up shows an e-like upper enclosed counter and horizontal middle bar together with a lower a bowl and right foot in the same cell after l. The article is not a clean single a. Both e/a layer orders are retained; the source grammar does not settle their chronology.
- M6, after premier à: `\unsure{Y|\zeta}`. The forked upper shape supports Y, while the lower curling return also permits zeta. Y remains primary; no replacement by an expected characteristic symbol is made.

### Remaining uncertainties

- 1, whole display: `\deleted{F} before pen f^*`. Additional type upright/serifs are visible under the correction; full identity is obscured. All neighboring signs, brackets, indices and shifts were read together. The second upright/middle-bar remnants also admit H.
- 4, canceled reference: `4.3`. Lower curve favors 3, but x overprinting masks the last numeral; no cancellation doubt is resolved without all strokes.
- 6: `\deleted{du}`. d is clearer; second canceled cell cannot distinguish u/e securely.
- 9: `\deleted{e}t in foncteurs`. t ascender/crossbar and e bowl/middle bar share one cell. Their order is not established by grammar. A rounded o under t is also possible because the crossbar can belong to t itself.
- 10, initial quotation boundary: `\deleted{(} before double quotation`. A long parenthesis-like curve remains below the high quote strokes. Overlap does not prove whether the curve is canceled or live.
- 11, second cell of opening word: `\deleted{q}n`. Clear n arch/legs plus extra descender material. The right-side descender and foot favor q; the C p candidate remains a weaker possibility. Layer order remains uncertain for either identity.
- 21: `\deleted{d}\handwritten{q}ae`. High d material and low q descender are both visible; order uncertain. The middle a itself is clear and not normalized to u.
- 22, above first a: `short horizontal stroke, encoded - above a`. Foreground-dark detached short horizontal aligned above the a; its function and original technique are unresolved.
- 22, below first a: `*`. Crossed mark with downward arms below the a resembles lower pen stars elsewhere. Whether it is a correction remnant or annotation is uncertain; placement remains explicit.
- 25, underlying list letter: `\deleted{a}`. Old type is largely hidden by the heavy pen d; remaining bowl fragments do not certify every part of a.
- above 28: `contient`. Final tall crossed t and i dot are clear, but compact initial and middle joins leave the full word provisional. H entraîne does not account for the final t.
- 5, initial cell before identifie: `\unsure{\deleted{d}s|\deleted{s}d}'identifie`. The first cell before the apostrophe has a tall right upright with a leftward top serif, a closed lower bowl, and the separate upper/lower curves of s. A clean s omits the d-shaped ascender and bowl. Both d/s orders are retained because the superposition does not securely establish chronology.
- 24, final word: `form\unsure{\deleted{m}u|\deleted{u}m|m}laire`. The fifth cell has two upper shoulders and extra uprights, together with a crowded rounded lower join. These strokes do not fit a clean u. The visible m-shaped impression is preserved, with m/u and u/m overprint orders and a single m as alternatives; the expected spelling does not choose the layer order.
- 28, after que,: `\unsure{\deleted{t}v|\deleted{v}t}ia`. The first cell has the v diagonals and lower meeting point together with a separate tall upright and crossbar of t. Both are in the same type position. Their order is unresolved, but plain v omits the extra ascender and crossbar.
- 28, final formule: `form\unsure{\deleted{m}u|\deleted{u}m|m}le`. The fifth cell has two upper shoulders and extra uprights, together with a crowded rounded lower join. These strokes do not fit a clean u. The visible m-shaped impression is preserved, with m/u and u/m overprint orders and a single m as alternatives; the expected spelling does not choose the layer order.

### Remaining uncertainties

- 1: `p\textsuperscript{\handwritten{\unsure{/}}}\unsure{\deleted{a}o|\deleted{o}a}\unsure{\deleted{r}u|\deleted{u}r|\deleted{n}u|\deleted{u}n}r`. Both middle cells visibly carry more strokes than an ordinary single impression. The high thin stroke is source ink but its identity/attachment is not established.
- 6: `\deleted{\unsure{le ca}}`. The x cancellation obscures portions of each short word; retain the existing C doubt.
- 7: `\unsure{\deleted{d}r|\deleted{r}d}ecommand\unsure{\deleted{a}é|\deleted{é}a}`. The first high ascender and loop are separate from the r shoulder; the final a-like baseline foot/counter is more congested. The page marks both layers uncertain.
- 8: `\deleted{\unsure{b|a}}`. The new a covers part of the old stem and bowl. The independent label detail also allows a: an upper small counter and lower bowl lie inside the larger pen a, but the earlier stem is largely covered. Old b/a remains uncertain.
- 8: `\unsure{\deleted{g}f|\deleted{f}g}`. Both g's lower loop and f's high loop/crossbar are visible. Their order is not established from the shared black ink.
- 11: `\deleted{\unsure{c|a|b}}`. The new b obscures much of the old typed curve. The two small internal counter fragments also allow old a or b beneath the new pen b; c remains primary, and none is secure.
- 11: `l'\unsure{\deleted{g}h|\deleted{h}g|\deleted{j}h|\deleted{h}j}omomorphisme`. The descender loop cannot be part of an ordinary h. A g is the strongest candidate, without secure chronology. The raised right-hand blob and lower hooked return also allow j beneath/over h. The blob is joined to the upper shoulder, so the image does not securely distinguish a j dot from the ear of g; both j/h orders are added beside g/h.
- 12: `\deleted{\unsure{d|c}}`. The pen c crosses the typed ascender/loop. The low rounded fragment also permits an earlier c; d/c remains uncertain because the possible ascender is covered.
- 15: `\deleted{\unsure{e|d}}`. The new f and crossing ink obscure the base character. The low rounded type remnant and obscured right side also permit d; e/d remains uncertain.
- 18: `\deleted{\unsure{f}}`. The new e obscures the earlier stem/crossing.
- 18: `\unsure{\deleted{a}A|\deleted{A}a}nneau`. The high pointed A and lower rounded a-shaped ink coexist; order is not secure.
- 21: `\deleted{\unsure{S|et|S'|St}}`. The x-overwritten interval preserves rounded and upright fragments but not enough unoccluded strokes to choose. The tall right remnant permits t and the short upper trace permits a prime; S-prime and St are admitted beside S/et without resolving the x-covered letters.
- 27: `appl\unsure{\deleted{j}i|\deleted{i}j}quant`. The dotted stem/baseline foot and descending hook overlap. The upper short stroke is congested; chronology remains uncertain.
- 27: `\deleted{\unsure{la for}}`. Not all letters survive the typed x cancellation independently.
- 32: `\unsure{\deleted{2}1|\deleted{1}2|\deleted{7}1|\deleted{1}7}7`. The first numeral has overlapping stem/base and slanted/curved strokes. The 7 is clear. No reference was looked up. The upper horizontal plus descending diagonal also permits7 in the covered first cell; both7/1 orders are added beside2/1. The final7 and three separate stops are unchanged.

### Remaining uncertainties

- L03–L04: `\deleted{\unsure{et n'a|et nta} guère de sens que dans}`. The cancellation and right paper edge obscure the last letter of the first group. The lower continuation is clear; the entire group is not promoted to certainty. The high upright in the penultimate cell also permits t; no clipped letter is completed.
- L05: `\deleted{m}\handwritten{\unsure{f|l}}\unsure{\deleted{u}i|\deleted{i}u}néa\unsure{r|\deleted{i}r|\deleted{r}i}\unsure{\deleted{é}i|\deleted{i}é|í}té`. The first cell has two typed m shoulders/three uprights beneath a taller looped pen f/l. The next has an i dot and upright together with a u-shaped lower outline. These are simultaneous letter impressions, not merely alternative spellings. n-é-a and t-é remain clear. The r cell has a crowded extra upper projection, so plain r remains primary with i/r layers possible. The next cell has a tall i-like stem plus a low rounded body and an acute: é/i layers or an accented i remain possible. The first typed m and second-cell layers are restored without imposing the expected word linéarité.
- L12: `\deleted{\unsure{s|e}}`. Heavy x strike prevents deciding the small original terminal cell after spécial.
- L23, recurring H exponents also on L27: `\unsure{*|+}`. First exponent has a strong cross with a small extra upper-right branch. Other H exponents have clearer additional short strokes. Mark the recurring doubt at the first occurrence only and use primary * for the matching later marks.
- A4 / L26: `\neq\unsure{\phi|\varnothing}`. The added glyph has a long nearly upright stem through a narrow open oval; this favors phi in the literal transcription. Meaning of the condition does not decide the shape.
- L27: `\handwritten{\unsure{$\leftarrow$}}`. Short shaft plus left-facing dark wedge is visible; its editorial function or state cannot be inferred. The separate comma below/right is clear.
- L01, apostrophe/e cell: `d\unsure{\deleted{'}e|\deleted{e}'}`. High apostrophe-like upright overlaps the lower e counter. Their order is not established by the ink.
- L26, des after pgcd: `\unsure{\deleted{d}|\deleted{x}d|\deleted{d}x}es`. The d after pgcd has its tall right stem and bowl crossed by two diagonals occupying the same type cell; the following e and s are live. The crossed d is primary. An x under a restored d, or the reverse order, cannot be excluded because the impression order is not separable. A plain unqualified des omits the crossing layer.

### Remaining uncertainties

- 5: `\handwritten{\unsure{\longrightarrow|\Longrightarrow}}`. The left shaft is a narrow two-track loop but converges into a single shaft toward the head. Retraced single arrow is first; double arrow is not excluded.
- 6: `\deleted{\unsure{qui, pour $f$}}`. Typed x cancellation hides portions of the run and punctuation. Retained C doubt.
- 9: `l'interp\unsure{\deleted{é}r|\deleted{r}é}éter`. The cell after interp has both an r shoulder and accented-e contour, with the following clear é in its own position. Chronology is uncertain.
- 11: `\handwritten{\unsure{,|)}}`. A single long downward curve is visible, not two secure punctuation marks. C's comma is first; no punctuation is added to balance the expression.
- 11: `\deleted{\text{\unsure{sur|noté}}\ \unsure{\phi_Y|Y}}`. The old run remains crossed under the inserted varphi/membership/H. Rounded middle remnants can support sur or noté, but the added strokes prevent secure word segmentation. At the right, the low serifed upright and divided upper arms support Y, with a phi-like oval/stem above it; the independent f proposal has no separately exposed f shoulder here. The original phi_Y/Y alternatives remain, and noté is added only as a qualification.
- 13: `\handwritten{\unsure{\in|\leftarrow}}`. The strike crosses the relation's middle; curved returns support membership, but an arrowhead cannot be excluded. The section wording does not remove the cancellation doubt.
- 13: `\underline{\mathrm{H}}^0\unsure{(}\mathrm{R}f^{!}`. A distinct curve stands between the upper0 and R. The writing is retained without filling a missing closing parenthesis.
- 14: `\unsure{(\deleted{\unsure{\mathrm{R}}}|\deleted{(\unsure{\mathrm{R}}}}`. The curved opening stroke immediately before the congested R-shaped capital is visible, but its lower part meets the same cancellation strokes. Both a live opening curve followed by canceled R and an opening curve included in the cancellation remain possible. The capital identity itself stays doubtful; no parentheses are balanced by inference.
- 19: `\unsure{\deleted{Y}S|\deleted{S}Y|\deleted{g}S|\deleted{S}g|\deleted{S}S}`. The S-shaped outer body has an extra upper counter/crossing and a lower inner stroke. The existing Y/S and g/S layer candidates remain possible; a second displaced S also fits the doubled bowls. None of these alternatives establishes temporal order.
- 20: `clas\unsure{\deleted{s}e|\deleted{e}s}s`. Only six keyed positions are present. The fifth contains s/e overlap. A separate seventh letter is not restored.
- 23: `\deleted{\unsure{\underline{\mathrm{H}}^i(\mathrm{R}|\underline{\mathrm{H}}^i\mathrm{R}}}`. Several diagonal strikes obscure the possible parenthesis and parts of the capitals. Both original C candidates remain.
- 26: `c\unsure{\deleted{q}a|\deleted{a}q}s`. The middle cell has an a-like body and q-like descender; order uncertain.
- 26: `e\unsure{s|\deleted{t}s|\deleted{s}t|\deleted{s}s}t`. The enlarged est cell shows a normal s body with two offset dark fragments along its right side, where the following t crossbar approaches. No independent tall t ascender is exposed in the s cell. The first t/s interpretation is therefore qualified by a repeated-s impression and by a single s with a displaced ink edge. Plain est is not excluded; neither witness is counted as changed at this cell.
- 6, after closing bracket: `\handwritten{]}\handwritten{\unsure{/.|b}}`. The enlarged right edge separates the closing bracket from a second rising slant to its right. That slant runs down into or beside a small low rounded mark. The first reading had assigned the slant to the bracket, but a clear white strip separates them. Slash plus stop is primary; a b-like letter remains possible where the upper stroke meets the black image edge. The additional mark is transcribed as text, not a drawing.
- 23, relation: `\handwritten{\unsure{<|\leq}}`. The main sign has two oblique branches meeting at a left vertex and a long lower-right return, favoring <. Heavy overtracing hides its inner region, so a covered short lower bar cannot be ruled out; <= remains secondary. No equality pair and slash support H non-equality. The separate crowded coefficient is recorded in R029.
- 23, coefficient after the inequality: `\unsure{\deleted{\mathrm{R}}2|\deleted{2}\mathrm{R}|\deleted{1}2|\deleted{2}1|\deleted{2}2}`. The coefficient cell after the relation contains the curved upper-left and baseline strokes of2 together with a straighter inner upright, a right bowl/leg and a small lower white counter that a single clean2 does not account for. R/2 layers are primary; a1-like underlying stroke or a repeated2 remain possible. The temporal order is not separable, so both R/2 and1/2 orders are retained. The extra impression is recorded without choosing the mathematically expected coefficient.

### Remaining uncertainties

- 1: `\deleted{a}c`. Outer c-like curve and a-like closed lower bowl share a single type position. Layer order cannot be determined.
- 4, final cell of classe: `\deleted{\textsuperscript{u}}e`. Two raised uprights with a joined lower turn sit over the readable e. A raised u-like layer and a diacritic interpretation remain possible; both chronological orders are unproved.
- 5, canceled token: `de`. d is clear. x cancellation merges with the distinguishing middle/bowl strokes of the second cell.
- 6, whole display (4.8): `\deleted{\handwritten{\phi}_f}\handwritten{\gamma_{X/Y}\in}\mathrm{H}^{\handwritten{2}(d-d')}_{\unsure{\inserted{\handwritten{X}}|\deleted{\unsure{X|Y}}\handwritten{X}}}(\unsure{\handwritten{Y}|\handwritten{X}|\deleted{\unsure{X|Y}}\handwritten{Y}},A_{\unsure{\handwritten{Y}|\deleted{\unsure{X|Y}}\handwritten{Y}}}(d-d')).`. The complete formula and D004–D007 show the old phi/f beneath the added gamma; the new index has crossing diagonals for X, while the old typed f belongs to the canceled notation. At the support index, first argument and A index, angular typed serifs and pen contours overlap. They may be a changed X/Y or a retraced same letter; the covered original cannot be recovered securely. Each earlier unlayered reading remains admitted, with possible old X/Y layers added as qualifications. No additional old letter is asserted as certain. Ordinary H, raised2, both d-d-prime orders and the final stop remain literal.
- 12, canceled five-cell zone: `\deleted{aux \unsure{e}}`. Final cell has e-like bowl/middle residual beyond the cancellation. Its exact letter and the original spacing are uncertain. Fourth x-only cell is cancellation fill; no additional source x is asserted.
- 14, live marked e: `d\unsure{è|é|e|\deleted{'}e|\deleted{t}e|\deleted{e}'|\deleted{e}t}\deleted{en}`. The high stroke merges with the upper edge of the live e. An accent, apostrophe-like impression, or t-like overstrike cannot be distinguished. The earlier è/é/e alternatives remain and both possible layer orders are added. Two distinct following cells retain e and n below the cancellation; both en cells stay canceled.
- 18, punctuation after raisonnable: `canceled ;`. A lower descender favors semicolon, but the heavy crossing prevents full separation of the original punctuation.
- 27, Compatibilité cell: `Compatibi\unsure{\deleted{m}l|\deleted{l}m|\deleted{n}l|\deleted{l}n}ité`. The crowded cell has a tall serifed l and low arch/leg material. Two arches favor m, but their merger with the tall stem prevents ruling out n. Both m/l orders remain, and both n/l orders are added without removing the original alternatives.
- 27, class symbol: `\unsure{\underline{\handwritten{\phi}}|\handwritten{\phi}}_f`. The horizontal foot may be an underline or an integral/residual return of phi. Both underlined and plain phi remain admitted; no independent old letter is securely recoverable, and this uncertainty alone is not a C reading change.
- 27, initial cell before es: `\deleted{v}c`. Curved c body and pointed v diagonals coexist in one type cell; order is uncertain.
- 28: `\unsure{\handwritten{,}|\handwritten{;}|\deleted{;}|\deleted{,}|\deleted{.}}`. The final small mark has a high dark component and two diverging lower edges. Comma/semicolon remains plausible, but one crossing can also be a cancellation over semicolon, comma or stop. The crop does not separate these layers or establish that the mark is live. The original two readings remain alongside cancellation alternatives; no punctuation is supplied from grammar.
- 3, canceled three-cell token: `\deleted{d\unsure{u|e}\unsure{n|s}}`. Three canceled type positions remain distinct. The first has a clear d ascender and bowl. In the second and third, keyed x crosses the distinguishing middle strokes: u/e and n/s cannot be separated completely. All three positions are canceled; the second reader proposal of live later letters is not supported by a separate uncrossed baseline. The primary dun is retained with local letter alternatives.
- 14, first propriétés: `propri\unsure{\deleted{t}é|\deleted{é}t}tés`. After the separate i, one crowded cell has the bowl/middle stroke and acute of é together with a taller t-like upright and crossing. A further clear t, then é and s follow. A single clean é cannot account for the additional ascender within the preceding cell. The overlapping é/t forms are retained in both orders; no chronology is inferred.
- 18, before replacement parenthesis: `\deleted{\unsure{;|:}}\handwritten{)}:`. The old punctuation lies under the replacement closing-parenthesis curve, with a distinct live colon to its right. Its lower tailed material favors semicolon, but merges with the new curve; colon is therefore admitted as a secondary old-punctuation reading. The placement correction remains.
- 20, inserted example: `\inserted{\handwritten{\unsure{p.ex.|p.cx.} $\unsure{G|C}=(A)_Y$,}}`. The added abbreviation has p, a stop, a small open middle letter, x and a stop. A faint middle bar favors e, but c remains possible. The following capital has a C-like open body and short right foot favoring G; G/C are retained locally. Equality, both parentheses, A, lower Y and final comma are clear. The carrier still attaches after S comma and before alors.

### Remaining uncertainties

- 2: `\unsure{\deleted{c}C|\deleted{C}c|\deleted{o}C|\deleted{C}o}ompatibilité`. The composite has a full-height C with upper and lower terminals, plus a lower rounded body. That lower body can be c with a right terminal masked by the capital, or o with its right arc merged into the capital. Both earlier c/C orders remain and both o/C orders are added. A single heavy capital still fails to account for the lower contour.
- 4: `\deleted{f}g`. High hooked f and low looped g occupy one label cell; their order is unresolved.
- 4: `\handwritten{($S\unsure{\neg|\text{---}|\longrightarrow}T$)}`. The entire S-to-T group shows a horizontal shaft ending in a short downward perpendicular stroke, resembling neg. There is no separately forked arrowhead. The neg-shaped sign is recorded literally, without assigning logical-negation meaning; the earlier plain-shaft and arrow readings remain alternatives.
- 6: `Re\deleted{m}lation`. m shoulders and l ascender are both present in the third cell; typed order is unknown.
- 9: `d\unsure{\deleted{u}e|\deleted{e}u|\deleted{a}u|\deleted{u}a} par.4`. The second type cell after d has an upper rounded compartment, a middle horizontal stroke and a lower rounded/vertical return. The middle bar favors e over u, while the closed lower contour and right foot also admit a/u overlap. Both e/u orders remain and both a/u orders are added; par.4 and its following comma remain clear.
- 11: `trivial\deleted{\unsure{,|e|.}}`. After the intact final l there is a low x cancellation over a residual curved mark. Its low hooked part supports a comma; the rounded upper residue could be e but is inseparable from the x. Both original alternatives remain. The mark is not omitted or made live. A small stop can also account for the low residual when merged with x; it is added as a secondary alternative.
- 16: `pre\deleted{d}n\deleted{n}dre`. The two crowded n/d cells are independent. Do not flatten the physical composite to either a clean preddre or a clean prendre.
- 17: `énoncé\deleted{\unsure{,|(}}`. A small canceled punctuation cell immediately follows the accented final é. A low comma-like residue survives under the x. The much taller downstroke to its right belongs to the insertion carrier, which can be followed beneath the entire existence parenthesis. The punctuation remains doubtful and struck. The left residue also has a higher curved beginning that can be a canceled opening parenthesis. That alternative is retained separately from the taller insertion carrier.
- 20: `pr\unsure{\deleted{i}u|\deleted{u}i}\unsure{d|\deleted{u}d|\deleted{d}u|\deleted{a}d|\deleted{d}a}ent`. The third cell clearly has the centered i dot and u body. The following d has a high ascender and bowl with extra inner diagonals/counters. Their merger permits an earlier u or a as well as a single crowded d; both possible layer orders are stated without excluding the original plain d.
- 22: `2\unsure{\deleted{p}\handwritten{\delta}|\handwritten{p}|\deleted{)}\handwritten{p}}`. After2 the added high bent stroke merges with the typed p bowl. The original p/delta possibilities remain; a canceled closing curve beneath a retraced p is also admitted, as proposed by the second reading. The nearby square brackets have gray inner and dark outer retraced edges. Since a plain p reading is still admitted, this place is a certainty qualification, not a C/H reading change.
- 23: `\deleted{g}\handwritten{f}`. The g loop/descender and f hook/crossbar coexist; the apparent handwritten f is preferred but chronology is not fully separable.
- 25: `(4.\unsure{\deleted{9}5|\deleted{5}9|\deleted{4}5|\deleted{5}4})`. The composite final digit has the top bar/left stem and lower curve of5 together with extra material closing upper/right compartments. The original9/5 layer readings remain. A pointed inner counter and upright can also belong to4/5 layers, so both orders are added. The extra foreground material does not support a single clean5; no preferred mathematical reference resolves the overprint.
- 28–29: `l’homomorphi\unsure{s}me`. The left upper and lower turns of the final s are visible at the black edge; the next physical line begins me. This is a new certainty doubt only, not a substantive C/H replacement.
- 19, canceled prefix: `\deleted{\unsure{au|en} prélimi}`. The final dotted i after the separate m remains visible, so prélimi is retained. In the first two heavily canceled cells, the lower bowl and middle remnant can support a/e, while the following shoulder/cup is merged with x. Both au and en are admitted as whole-prefix alternatives. The first-stage assertion that the prefix was fully separated is withdrawn.
- 22, first twist sign: `f^*(G)(\inserted{\handwritten{\unsure{-|=}}}p)`. Before the first p the principal dark horizontal stroke has a second lighter nearly parallel track above it. Retraced minus is primary, but equality-shaped ink cannot be excluded. The mathematical role favors minus but does not remove the visible alternative. The first p remains unoverwritten.
